X
. Assume that there are smooth and
bounded functions
\eta
,
g>0
on
X
such that the (Hermitian)
curvature operator

\textbf{B}:=[\eta\sqrt{-1}\Theta_{E}-\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar{\partial}\eta-\sqrt{-1}g\partial\eta\wedge\bar{\partial}\eta,\Lambda_{\omega}]

is positive definite everywhere on
\Lambda^{n,q}T^{*}_{X}\otimes E
, for some
q\geq 1
.
Then for every form
\lambda\in L^{2}(X,\Lambda^{n,q}T^{*}_{X}\otimes E)
such that
D^{\prime\prime}\lambda=0
and

\int_{X}\langle\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda,\lambda\rangle dV_{M}<\infty
,
there exists
u\in L^{2}(X,\Lambda^{n,q-1}T^{*}_{X}\otimes E)
such that
D^{\prime\prime}u=\lambda
and

\int_{X}(\eta+g^{-1})^{-1}|u|^{2}dV_{M}\leq\int_{X}\langle\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda,\lambda\rangle dV_{M}.

Lemma 7.3
.

(see
[
25
]
)Let
X
and
E
be as in the above lemma and

\theta
be a continuous
(1,0)
form on
X
. Then we have

[\sqrt{-1}\theta\wedge\bar{\theta},\Lambda_{\omega}]\alpha=\bar{\theta}\wedge(\alpha\llcorner(\bar{\theta})^{\sharp}\big),

for any
(n,1)
form
\alpha
with value in
E
. Moreover, for any
positive
(1,1)
form
\beta
, we have
[\beta,\Lambda_{\omega}]
is
semipositive.

The following Lemma belongs to Fornaess
and Narasimhan on approximation property of plurisubharmonic
functions of Stein manifolds.

Lemma 7.4
.

[
14
]
Let
X
be a Stein manifold and
\varphi\in PSH(X)
. Then there exists a sequence

\{\varphi_{n}\}_{n=1,2,\cdots}
of smooth strongly plurisubharmonic functions such that

\varphi_{n}\downarrow\varphi
.

For the sake of completeness, let’s recall some steps in the proof in

[
18
]
(see also
[
23
,
25
,
27
]
) with some slight
modifications in order to prove Lemma
2.1
.

It follows from Remark
7.1
that
it suffices to consider that
M
is a Stein manifold, and
F
is holomorphic
(n,0)
form on
\{\psi<-t_{0}\}
and

\int_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}<+\infty,

(7.3)

and there exist smooth plurisubharmonic functions
\psi_{m}
and
\varphi_{m}
on
M
decreasingly convergent to
\psi
and
\varphi
respectively,
satisfying
\sup_{m}\sup_{M}\psi_{m}<-T
and
\sup_{m}\sup_{M}\varphi_{m}<+\infty
.

Step 1: construct some functions

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{8}B)
.
Let
\{v_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{8}B)}
be a family of smooth increasing convex functions on
\mathbb{R}
,
which are continuous functions on
\mathbb{R}\cup\{-\infty\}
, such that:

(1)

v_{\varepsilon}(t)=t
for
t\geq-t_{0}-\varepsilon
,
v_{\varepsilon}(t)=constant
for
t<-t_{0}-B+\varepsilon
and are pointwise convergent to
v_{t_{0},B}(t)
;

(2)

v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)
are pointwise convergent to
\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B,-t_{0})}
, when
\varepsilon\to 0
,
and
0\leq v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)\leq\frac{2}{B}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B+\varepsilon,-t_{0}-\varepsilon)}
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
;

(3)

v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)
are pointwise convergent to
b_{t_{0},B}(t)
which is a continuous function on
\mathbb{R}
, when
\varepsilon\to 0
, and
0\leq v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)\leq 1
for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

One can construct the family
\{v_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in(0,\frac{1}{8}B)}
by the setting

\begin{split}v_{\varepsilon}(t):=&\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{B-4\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B+2\varepsilon,-t_{0}-2\varepsilon)}*\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon}\right)(s)ds\right)dt_{1}\\
&-\int_{-\infty}^{-t_{0}}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{B-4\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B+2\varepsilon,-t_{0}-2\varepsilon)}*\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon}\right)(s)ds\right)dt_{1}-t_{0},\end{split}

(7.4)

where
\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon}
is the kernel of convolution satisfying
supp(\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon})\subset(-\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon,\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon)
.
Then it follows that

v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\frac{1}{B-4\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B+2\varepsilon,-t_{0}-2\varepsilon)}*\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon}(t),

and

v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{B-4\varepsilon}\mathbb{I}_{(-t_{0}-B+2\varepsilon,-t_{0}-2\varepsilon)}*\rho_{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon}\right)(s)ds.

Let
\eta=s(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
and
\phi=u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
,
where
s\in C^{\infty}((S,+\infty))
satisfies
s>0
and
s^{\prime}>0
, and

u\in C^{\infty}((S,+\infty))
, such that
u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}>0
, and
s^{\prime}-u^{\prime}s=1
.
It follows from
\sup_{m}\sup_{M}\psi_{m}<-S
and
\max{\{t,-t_{0}-B\}}\leq v_{\epsilon}(t)\leq\max{\{t,t_{0}\}}
that
\phi=u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
are uniformly bounded
on
M
with respect to
m
and
\varepsilon
,
and
u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))
are uniformly bounded
on
M
with respect to
\varepsilon
.
Let
\Phi=\phi+\varphi_{m^{\prime}}
,
and let
\tilde{h}=e^{-\Phi}
.

Let
f(x)=2\mathbb{I}_{(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})}*\rho(x)
be a smooth function on
\mathbb{R}
, where
\rho
is is the kernel of convolution satisfying
supp(\rho)\subset(-\frac{1}{3},\frac{1}{3})
and
\rho\geq 0
.

Let
g_{l}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}lf(lx)&\mbox{if}&x\leq 0\\
lf(l^{2}x)&\mbox{if}&x>0\end{array}\right.
, then
\{g_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
be a family of smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}
satisfying that:

(1)

supp(g_{l})\subset[-\frac{1}{l},\frac{1}{l}],

g_{l}(x)\geq 0
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
;

(2)

\int_{-\frac{1}{l}}^{0}g_{l}(x)dx=1
,
\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{l}}g_{l}(x)dx\leq\frac{1}{l}
for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
.

Set
c_{l}(t)=e^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(e^{y}(t-S)+S)g_{l}(y)dy
, where
h(t)=e^{-t}c(t)
and
c\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{S}
. It is easy to get

c_{l}(t)-c(t)\geq e^{t}\int_{-\frac{1}{l}}^{0}(h(e^{y}(t-S)+S)-h(t))g_{l}(y)dy\geq 0.

Set
\tilde{h}(t)=h(e^{t}+S)
and
\tilde{g}_{l}(t)=g_{l}(-t)
, then
c_{l}(t)=e^{t}\tilde{h}*\tilde{g}_{l}(\ln(t-S))\in C^{\infty}(S,+\infty)
. Because
h(t)
is decreasing with respect to
t
, so is
c_{l}(t)e^{-t}
. And

\begin{split}\int_{S}^{s}c_{l}(t)e^{-t}dt&=\int_{S}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(e^{y}(t-S)+S)g_{l}(y)dydt\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)\int_{S}^{e^{y}(s-S)+S}h(t)dtdy\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)dy\int_{S}^{e(s-S)+S}h(t)dt\\
&<+\infty,\end{split}

then
c_{l}(t)\in\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{S}
for any
l\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
.

As
h(t)
is decreasing with respect to
t
, then set
h^{-}(t)=\lim_{s\rightarrow t-0}h(s)\geq h(t)
and
c^{-}(t)=\lim_{s\rightarrow t-0}c(s)\geq c(t)
, then we claim that
\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}c_{l}(t)=c^{-}(t)
. In fact, we have

\begin{split}|c_{l}(t)-c^{-}(t)|\leq&e^{t}\int_{-\frac{1}{l}}^{0}|h(e^{y}(t-S)+S)-h^{-}(t)|g_{l}(y)dy\\
&+e^{t}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{l}}h(e^{y}(t-S)+S)g_{l}(y)dy.\end{split}

(7.5)

\forall\varepsilon>0
,
\exists\delta>0
and
|h(t-\delta)-h^{-}(t)|<\varepsilon
. Then
\exists N>0
,
\forall l>N
, such that
e^{y}(t-S)+S>t-\delta
for all
y\in[-\frac{1}{l},0)
and
\frac{1}{l}<\varepsilon
. It following from (
7.5
) that

|c_{l}(t)-c^{-}(t)|\leq\varepsilon e^{t}+\varepsilon h(t)e^{t},

hence
\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}c_{l}(t)=c^{-}(t)
for any
t>S
.

Step 2: Solving
\bar{\partial}-
equation with smooth polar function and smooth weight

Now let
\alpha\in\Lambda_{x}^{n,1}T_{M}^{*}
, for any
x\in M
. Using inequality
s>0
and the fact that
\varphi_{m}
is
plurisubharmonic on
M
, we get

\begin{split}\langle\textbf{B}\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}=&\langle[\eta\sqrt{-1}\Theta_{\tilde{h}}-\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar{\partial}\eta-\sqrt{-1}g\partial\eta\wedge\bar{\partial}\eta,\Lambda_{\omega}]\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\\
\geq&\langle[\eta\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar{\partial}\phi-\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar{\partial}\eta-\sqrt{-1}g\partial\eta\wedge\bar{\partial}\eta,\Lambda_{\omega}]\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}.\end{split}

(7.6)

where
g>0
is a smooth and bounded function on
M
.
We need the following calculations to determine
g
.

\begin{split}&\partial\bar{\partial}\eta=-s^{\prime}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\partial\bar{\partial}(v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))+s^{\prime\prime}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\partial v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})\wedge\bar{\partial}v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}),\end{split}

(7.7)

and

\begin{split}&\partial\bar{\partial}\phi=-u^{\prime}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\partial\bar{\partial}v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})+u^{\prime\prime}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\partial v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})\wedge\bar{\partial}v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}).\end{split}

(7.8)

Then we have

\begin{split}&-\partial\bar{\partial}\eta+\eta\partial\bar{\partial}\phi-g(\partial\eta)\wedge\bar{\partial}\eta\\
=&(s^{\prime}-su^{\prime})\partial\bar{\partial}v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})+((u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime})-gs^{\prime 2})\partial(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\bar{\partial}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\\
=&(s^{\prime}-su^{\prime})(v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})\partial\bar{\partial}\psi_{m}+v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})\partial(\psi_{m})\wedge\bar{\partial}(\psi_{m}))\\
&+((u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime})-gs^{\prime 2})\partial(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\wedge\bar{\partial}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})).\end{split}

(7.9)

We omit composite item
-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})
after
s^{\prime}-su^{\prime}
and
(u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime})-gs^{\prime 2}
in the above equalities. Let
g=\frac{u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}}{s^{\prime 2}}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
.
It follows that
\eta+g^{-1}=(s+\frac{s^{\prime 2}}{u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}})(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
.

As
v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
and
s^{\prime}-su^{\prime}=1
, using Lemma

7.3
, equality (
7.9
) and inequality
(
7.6
), we obtain

\begin{split}\langle\textbf{B}\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}=&\langle[\eta\sqrt{-1}\Theta_{\tilde{h}}-\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar{\partial}\eta-\sqrt{-1}g\partial\eta\wedge\bar{\partial}\eta,\Lambda_{\omega}]\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\\
\geq&\langle[(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\sqrt{-1}\partial\psi_{m}\wedge\bar{\partial}\psi_{m},\Lambda_{\omega}]\alpha,\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\\
=&\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\bar{\partial}\psi_{m}\wedge(\alpha\llcorner(\bar{\partial}\psi_{m})^{\sharp}\big),\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}.\end{split}

(7.10)

Using the definition of contraction, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
the inequality (
7.10
), we have

\begin{split}|\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\bar{\partial}\psi_{m}\wedge\gamma,\tilde{\alpha}\rangle_{\tilde{h}}|^{2}=&|\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\gamma,\tilde{\alpha}\llcorner(\bar{\partial}\psi_{m})^{\sharp}\big\rangle_{\tilde{h}}|^{2}\\
\leq&\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\gamma,\gamma\rangle_{\tilde{h}}(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})|\tilde{\alpha}\llcorner(\bar{\partial}\psi_{m})^{\sharp}\big|_{\tilde{h}}^{2}\\
=&\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\gamma,\gamma\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\bar{\partial}\psi_{m}\wedge(\tilde{\alpha}\llcorner(\bar{\partial}\psi_{m})^{\sharp}\big),\alpha\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\\
\leq&\langle(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\gamma,\gamma\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\langle\textbf{B}\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\alpha}\rangle_{\tilde{h}},\end{split}

(7.11)

for any
(n,0)
form
\gamma
.

It follows from
s>0
and
\varphi_{m^{\prime}}
is strongly plurisubharmonic that
B
is positive definite everywhere on
\Lambda^{n,1}T_{M}^{*}
.
As
F
is holomorphic on
\{\psi<-t_{0}\}
and
Supp(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))\subset\{\psi<-t_{0}\}
,
then
\lambda:=\bar{\partial}[(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}]

is well-defined and smooth on
M
.

Taking
\gamma=F
, and

\tilde{\alpha}=\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda
,
note that
\tilde{h}=e^{-\Phi}
,
using inequality (
7.11
),
we have

\langle\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda,\lambda\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\leq v^{\prime\prime}_{t_{0},\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|\tilde{F}|^{2}e^{-\Phi}.

Then it follows that

\int_{M}\langle\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda,\lambda\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\leq\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{t_{0},\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|\tilde{F}|^{2}e^{-\Phi}.

Assume that we can choose
\eta
and
\phi
such that
e^{v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m}}e^{\phi}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})=(\eta+g^{-1})^{-1}
. Using Lemma
7.2
,
we have locally
L^{1}
function
u_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}
on
M
such that
\bar{\partial}u_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}=\lambda
,
and

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|u_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\\
=&\int_{M}|u_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}(\eta+g^{-1})^{-1}e^{-\Phi}\\
\leq&\int_{M}\langle\textbf{B}^{-1}\lambda,\lambda\rangle_{\tilde{h}}\\
\leq&\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|F|^{2}e^{-\Phi}\\
=&\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|F|^{2}e^{-\phi-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(7.12)

Let
F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}:=-u_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}+(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}
.
Then inequality (
7.12
) becomes

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\\
&\leq\int_{M}(v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))|F|^{2}e^{-\phi-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(7.13)

Step 3: Singular polar function and smooth weight

As
\sup_{m,\varepsilon}\sup_{M}|\phi|=\sup_{m,\varepsilon}|u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))|<+\infty
and
\sup_{M}\varphi_{m^{\prime}}<+\infty
,
note that

v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|F|^{2}e^{-\phi-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{2}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}\sup_{m,\varepsilon}e^{-\phi-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}

on
M
,
then it follows from inequality (
7.3
) and the dominated convergence theorem that

\lim_{m\to+\infty}\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})|F|^{2}e^{-\phi-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}=\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}

(7.14)

Note that
\inf_{m}\inf_{M}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})>0
,
then it follows from inequality (
7.13
) and (
7.14
)
that
\sup_{m}\int_{M}|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}|^{2}<+\infty
.
Note that

|(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))F|\leq|\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}F|,

(7.15)

then it follows from inequality (
7.3
)
that
\sup_{m}\int_{M}|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}<+\infty
,
which implies that there exists a subsequence of
\{F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}\}_{m}

(also denoted by
\{F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}\}_{m}
) compactly convergent to a holomorphic
F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}
on
M
.

Note that
e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})
are uniformly bounded on
M
with respect to
m
,
then it follows from

|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}|^{2}\leq 2(|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}+|(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}|^{2})\leq 2(|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}+|\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}F^{2}|)

and the dominated convergence theorem that

\begin{split}\lim_{m\to+\infty}&\int_{K}|F_{m,m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi_{m})\\
=&\int_{K}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)\end{split}

(7.16)

holds for any compact subset
K
on
M
.
Combining with inequality (
7.13
) and (
7.14
),
one can obtain that

\begin{split}&\int_{K}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)\\
&\leq\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}},\end{split}

(7.17)

which implies

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)\\
&\leq\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(7.18)

Step 4: Nonsmooth cut-off function

Note that

\sup_{\varepsilon}\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}<+\infty,

and

v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\leq\frac{2}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}\sup_{\varepsilon}\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}},

then it follows from inequality (
7.3
) and the dominated convergence theorem that

\begin{split}&\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\\
=&\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\\
\leq&(\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))})\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}<+\infty.\end{split}

(7.19)

Note that
\inf_{\varepsilon}\inf_{M}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)>0
,
then it follows from inequality (
7.18
) and (
7.19
) that

\sup_{\varepsilon}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}<+\infty.

Combining with

\sup_{\varepsilon}\int_{M}|(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}\leq\int_{M}\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}<+\infty,

(7.20)

one can obtain that
\sup_{\varepsilon}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}|^{2}<+\infty
,
which implies that
there exists a subsequence of
\{F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}\}_{\varepsilon\to 0}
(also denoted by
\{F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}\}_{\varepsilon\to 0}
)
compactly convergent to a holomorphic (n,0) form on
M
denoted by
F_{m^{\prime},l}
. Then it follows from inequality (
7.18
), inequality (
7.19
) and Fatou’s Lemma that

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v\circ\psi)\\
=&\int_{M}\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)\\
\leq&\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},\varepsilon,l}-(1-v^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}\circ\psi)\\
\leq&\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{M}v^{\prime\prime}_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|F|^{2}e^{-u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi))-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\\
\leq&(\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))})\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(7.21)

Step 5: Singular weight

Note that

\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\leq\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi}<+\infty,

(7.22)

and
\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))}<+\infty
,
then it from (
7.21
) that

\sup_{m^{\prime}}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},l}-(1-b(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v\circ\psi)<+\infty.

Combining with
\inf_{m^{\prime}}\inf_{M}e^{v(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v(\psi))>0
,
one can obtain that

\sup_{m^{\prime}}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},l}-(1-b(\psi)){F}|^{2}<+\infty.

Note that

\int_{M}|(1-b(\psi)){F}|^{2}\leq\int_{M}|\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}F|^{2}<+\infty.

(7.23)

Then
\sup_{m^{\prime}}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},l}|^{2}<+\infty
,
which implies that there exists a compactly convergent subsequence of
\{F_{m^{\prime},l}\}_{m^{\prime}}
(also denoted by
\{F_{m^{\prime},l}\}_{m^{\prime}}
),
which converges to a holomorphic (n,0) form
\tilde{F_{l}}
on
M
.
Then it follows from inequality (
7.21
), inequality (
7.22
) and Fatou’s Lemma that

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|F_{l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}\circ\psi)\\
=&\int_{M}\liminf_{m^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}|F_{m^{\prime},l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}\circ\psi)\\
\leq&\liminf_{m^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{M}|F_{m^{\prime},l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}\circ\psi)\\
\leq&\liminf_{m^{\prime}\rightarrow+\infty}(\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))})\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi_{m^{\prime}}}\\
\leq&(\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))})\int_{M}\frac{1}{B}\mathbb{I}_{\{-t_{0}-B<\psi<-t_{0}\}}|F|^{2}e^{-\varphi}.\end{split}

(7.24)

Step 6: ODE system

we need to find
\eta
and
\phi
such that

(\eta+g^{-1})=e^{-\psi_{m}}e^{-\phi}\frac{1}{c_{l}(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))}
on
M
and
s^{\prime}-u^{\prime}s=1
.
As
\eta=s(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
and
\phi=u(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
,
we have
(\eta+g^{-1})e^{v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m})}e^{\phi}=(s+\frac{s^{\prime 2}}{u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}})e^{-t}e^{u}\circ(-v_{\varepsilon}(\psi_{m}))
.

Summarizing the above discussion about
s
and
u
, we are naturally led to a
system of ODEs (see
[
22
,
23
,
25
,
27
]
):

\begin{split}&1).\,\,\left(s+\frac{s^{\prime 2}}{u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}}\right)e^{u-t}=\frac{1}{c_{l}(t)},\\
&2).\,\,s^{\prime}-su^{\prime}=1,\end{split}

(7.25)

where
t\in(T,+\infty)
.

It is not hard to solve the ODE system (
7.25
) and get
u(t)=-\log(\int_{S}^{t}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1})
and

s(t)=\frac{\int_{S}^{t}(\int_{S}^{t_{2}}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1})dt_{2}}{\int_{S}^{t}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}}

(see
[
25
]
).
It follows that
s\in C^{\infty}((S,+\infty))
satisfies
s>0
and
s^{\prime}>0
,

u\in C^{\infty}((S,+\infty))
satisfies
u^{\prime\prime}s-s^{\prime\prime}>0
.

As
u(t)=-\log(\int_{S}^{t}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1})
is decreasing with respect to
t
,
then it follows from
-S\geq v(t)\geq\max\{t,-t_{0}-B_{0}\}\geq-t_{0}-B_{0}
for any
t\leq 0

that

\begin{split}\sup_{M}e^{-u(-v(\psi))}\leq\sup_{t\in(S,t_{0}+B]}e^{-u(t)}=\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1},\end{split}

(7.26)

then it follows from inequality (
2.2
) and inequality (
7.24
) that

\int_{M}|F_{l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))\leq C\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}.

(7.27)

Step 7: non-smooth function
c

By the construction of
c_{l}
in Step
1
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}\\
=&\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}\int_{\mathbb{R}}h((t_{1}-S)e^{y}+S)g_{l}(y)dydt_{1}\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)\int_{S}^{(t_{0}+B-S)e^{y}+S}h(s)dsdy\\
=&\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)dy\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}h(s)ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)\int_{t_{0}+B}^{(t_{0}+B-S)e^{y}+S}h(s)dsdy.\end{split}

(7.28)

As

\begin{split}&\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)dy-1\right|\\
\leq&\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\left|\int_{-\frac{1}{l}}^{0}(e^{-y}-1)g_{l}(y)dy\right|+\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\left|\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{l}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)dy\right|\\
=&0\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-y}g_{l}(y)\int_{t_{0}+B}^{(t_{0}+B-S)e^{y}+S}h(s)dsdy\right|\\
\leq&e^{\frac{1}{l}}\left(1+\frac{1}{l}\right)h((t_{0}+B-S)e^{-1}+S)(t_{0}+B-S)(e^{\frac{1}{l}}-e^{-\frac{1}{l}}),\end{split}

then it follows from inequality (
7.28
) that

\lim_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}=\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}.

(7.29)

Combining with
\inf_{l}\inf_{M}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c_{l}(-v(\psi))\geq\inf_{M}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c(-v(\psi))>0
, we obtain that

\sup_{l}\int_{M}|F_{l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}<+\infty.

Note that

\int_{M}|(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}\leq\int_{M}|\mathbb{I}_{\{\psi<-t_{0}\}}F|^{2}<+\infty,

(7.30)

then
\sup_{l}\int_{M}|F_{l}|^{2}<+\infty
,
which implies that there exists a compactly convergent subsequence of
\{F_{l}\}
(also denoted by
\{F_{l}\}
),
which converges to a holomorphic (n,0) form
\tilde{F}
on
M
.
Then it follows from inequality (
7.27
) and the Fatou’s Lemma that

\begin{split}&\int_{M}|\tilde{F}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))\\
\leq&\int_{M}|\tilde{F}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c^{-}(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))\\
=&\int_{M}\liminf_{l\rightarrow+\infty}|F_{l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))\\
\leq&\liminf_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{M}|F_{l}-(1-b_{t_{0},B}(\psi)){F}|^{2}e^{v_{t_{0},B}(\psi)-\varphi}c_{l}(-v_{t_{0},B}(\psi))\\
\leq&C\liminf_{l\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c_{l}(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}\\
=&C\int_{S}^{t_{0}+B}c(t_{1})e^{-t_{1}}dt_{1}.\end{split}

Thus we prove Lemma
2.1
.

7.2.
Proof of Lemma
2.14

The proof is from
[
20
]
with a few minor modifications.

Choose
p\in suppT\cap U
. By Lemma
2.12
, there exist a real number
t>0
and a coordinate
(V,w)
, such that
w(p)=0
,
w(V)\cong B(0,1)
and
V\subset\subset\{G_{\Omega}(z,p)<-t\}\subset\subset U
. There exists a cut-off function
\theta
on
\Omega
, such that
\theta\equiv 1
on
w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{4}))
and
supp\theta\subset\subset w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
.

Let
\tilde{T}=\theta T
, then
\tilde{T}
is a closed positive
(1,1)
current on
\Omega
with

supp\tilde{T}\subset\subset w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
and
\tilde{T}\not\equiv 0
. Now, we prove that exists a subharmonic function
\Phi<0
on
\Omega
, which satisfies the following properties:
i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi=\tilde{T}
;
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+0}(\inf_{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\Phi(z))=0
;
\inf_{\Omega\backslash U}\Phi>-\infty
. Then
\Phi
satisfies the requirements in Lemma
2.14
.

Step 1: Construct
\Phi
.

Let
\rho\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{C})
be a function with

supp\rho\subset B(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
\rho(z)
depends only on

|z|
,
\rho\geq 0
and

\int_{\mathbb{C}}\rho(z)d\lambda_{z}=1
. Let

\rho_{n}(z)=n\rho(nz)
,
\rho_{n}
is a
family of smoothing kernels.

As
w(V)\cong B(0,1)
, without misunderstanding we see
(V,z_{1})
and
(B(0,1),w)
the same.
As
supp\tilde{T}\subset\subset w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
and
supp\rho\subset B(0,\frac{1}{2})
, denote that

T_{n}=\tilde{T}\ast\rho_{n}
be the convolution of
\tilde{T}
. In fact, for
any test function
h\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
,
((h\circ w^{-1})\ast\rho_{n})(w)
(
h\ast\rho_{n}(w)
for short) is well defined on
w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}))
, and

\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),h(z_{1})\rangle=<\tilde{T}(w),h\ast\rho_{n}(w)>
. Then
T_{n}
is a smooth closed positive
(1,1)
current on
\Omega
with

suppT_{n}\subset\subset w^{-1}(B(0,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2n}))
.

Let
u_{n}(z)=\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle
.
G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
is locally integrable with respect
z_{1}\in\Omega
for any fixed
z\in\Omega
implies that
u_{n}(z)>-\infty
for any
z\in\Omega
. For fixed
z
and fixed
n
, we
will prove
\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle=\langle\tilde{T}(w),(\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,\cdot)\ast\rho_{n})(w)\rangle
.
For fixed
z
,
G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
is a subharmonic function on
\Omega
.
There exists a sequence of smooth subharmonic functions

G_{m}(z_{1})
decreasingly converge to
G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
with respect
to
m
. As

G_{m}(z_{1})
is smooth, we have

\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{m}(z_{1})\rangle=<\tilde{T}(w),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{m}\ast\rho_{n}(w)>.

(7.31)

As
\tilde{T}
and
T_{n}
are closed positive
(1,1)
current
on
\Omega
with
suppT_{n}\subset\subset V
and
supp\tilde{T}\subset\subset V
, and
G_{m}(z_{1})

decreasingly converge to
G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})<0
with respect to
m
on
\Omega
, it
follows from Levi’s Theorem and equality (
7.31
) that

\begin{split}\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle=&\lim_{m\to+\infty}\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{m}(z_{!})\rangle\\
=&\lim_{m\to+\infty}<\tilde{T}(w),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{m}\ast\rho_{n}(w)>\\
=&<\tilde{T}(w),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,\cdot)\ast\rho_{n}(w)>.\end{split}

Fixed
z\in\Omega
, as
\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
is subharmonic, then
\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,\cdot)\ast\rho_{n}
is
decreasingly convergent to
\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
with respect to
n
.
Note that
\tilde{T}
is a positive
(1,1)
current on
\Omega
, then

u_{n}(z)
is decreasing with respect to
n
.
Let
\Phi(z)=\lim_{n\to+\infty}u_{n}(z)
.
G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})<0
on
\Omega\times\Omega
shows that
u_{n}(z)<0
and
\Phi(z)<0
on
\Omega
.

Step 2:
i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi=\tilde{T}
.

Firstly, we show that both
\{u_{n}\}
and
\Phi
is
L^{1}_{loc}

function on
\Omega
. As
u_{n}\leq 0
on
\Omega
and
u_{n}
is decreasingly convergent to
\Phi
with respect to
n
on
\Omega
, it suffices to prove that for any
q\in\Omega
there exists an open subset
K\subset\subset\Omega
, such that
q\in K
and
\int_{K}|u_{n}|dV_{\Omega}\leq C
, where
dV_{\Omega}
is some continuous volume form and
C
is a constant which independent of
n
.

It is clear that there exists a compact subset
D
of
V
such that
supp\tilde{T}\subset D
and
suppT_{n}\subset D
for any
n
. When
q\not\in V
, where exists a coordinate
w_{1}
on a neighborhood
V^{\prime}
of
q
, such that
w_{1}(q)=0
,
V^{\prime}\subset\subset\Omega
,
w_{1}(V^{\prime})\cong B(3,1)
and
\overline{V^{\prime}}\cap D=\emptyset
. Note that for any
(z,z_{1})\in V^{\prime}\times D
,
G(z,z_{1})<0
on
\Omega\times\Omega
,
G(z,z_{1})
is harmonic with respect to
z
or
z_{1}
when fixed another one and
\sum_{z_{1}\in V}|G(q,z_{1})|<+\infty
.
Without loss of generality, we see
(V^{\prime},z)
and
(B(3,1),w_{1})
the same and assume that
dV_{\Omega}=d\lambda_{z}
on
V^{\prime}
, where
d\lambda_{z}
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{C}
. Then we have

\begin{split}\int_{V^{\prime}}|u_{n}|dV_{\Omega}&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{z\in V^{\prime}}\int_{z_{1}\in V}|G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})|T_{n}(z_{1})d\lambda_{z}\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{z_{1}\in V}\int_{z\in V^{\prime}}|G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})|d\lambda_{z}T_{n}(z_{1})\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{z_{1}\in V}\pi|G_{\Omega}(q,z_{1})|T_{n}(z_{1})\\
&\leq\|T_{n}\|\sup_{z_{1}\in V}|G_{\Omega}(q,z_{1})|\\
&=\|\tilde{T}\|\sup_{z_{1}\in V}|G_{\Omega}(q,z_{1})|.\end{split}

(7.32)

When
q\in V
,
G_{\Omega}(w,\tilde{w})=\log|w-\tilde{w}|+v(w,\tilde{w})
on
V\times V
, where
v(w,\tilde{w})
is harmonic with respect to
w
or
\tilde{w}
when fixed another one. Without loss of generality, we see
(V,z)
and
(B(0,1),w)
the same and assume that
dV_{\Omega}=d\lambda_{w}
on
V
, where
d\lambda_{w}
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{C}
. Then we have

\begin{split}\int_{V}u_{n}dV_{\Omega}&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{w_{\in}V}\int_{\tilde{w}\in V}G_{\Omega}(w,\tilde{w})T_{n}(\tilde{w})d\lambda_{w}\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\tilde{w}\in V}\int_{w\in V}G_{\Omega}(w,\tilde{w})d\lambda_{w}T_{n}(\tilde{w})\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\tilde{w}\in V}\int_{w\in V}\log|w-\tilde{w}|d\lambda_{w}T_{n}(\tilde{w})+\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\tilde{w}\in V}\int_{w\in V}v(w,\tilde{w})d\lambda_{w}T_{n}(\tilde{w}).\end{split}

(7.33)

Note that

\int_{w\in V}\log|w-\tilde{w}|d\lambda_{w}\geq-\int_{w\in B(0,2)}|\log|w||d\lambda_{w}>-\infty

holds for any
\tilde{w}\in V
,

\int_{w\in V}v(w,\tilde{w})d\lambda_{w}=\pi v(q,\tilde{w})

holds for any
\tilde{w}\in V
and
\inf_{\tilde{w}\in V}v(q,\tilde{w})>-\infty
, then equality (
7.33
) implies that there exists a constant
N>0
such that

\int_{V}u_{n}dV_{\Omega}\geq N\|T_{n}\|.

(7.34)

By the definition of
T_{n}
, we know
\|T_{n}\|=\|\tilde{T}\|<+\infty
. As
u_{n}\leq 0
, combining inequality (
7.32
) and (
7.34
), we obtain that any
q\in\Omega
there exists an open subset
K\subset\subset\Omega
, such that
q\in K
and
\int_{K}|u_{n}|dV_{\Omega}\leq C
, where
dV_{\Omega}
is some continuous volume form and
C
is a constant which independent of
n
. Hence, we know
\{u_{n}\}\in L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)
and
\Phi\in L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)
.

Now we consider
i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi
. Let
g\in C^{\infty}_{c}(X)
be a test function. It follows from
\Phi\in L_{loc}^{1}(\Omega)
and the dominated convergence Theorem that

\begin{split}\langle i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi,g\rangle&=\langle\Phi(z),i\partial\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle\\
&=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\langle u_{n}(z),i\partial\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle.\end{split}

(7.35)

As
u_{n}(z)=\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle
, using Fubini’s Theorem, equality (
7.35
) becomes

\begin{split}\langle i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi,g\rangle&=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\langle\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle,i\partial\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle\\
&=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\langle\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1}),i\partial\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle\rangle.\end{split}

(7.36)

Since
T_{n}
is positive
(1,1)
current on
\Omega
,
T_{n}
converge weakly to
\tilde{T}
and
\frac{i}{\pi}\partial_{z}\bar{\partial}_{z}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})=\delta_{z_{1}}
, it follows from equality (
7.36
) that

\begin{split}\langle i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi,g\rangle&=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\langle\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1}),i\partial\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle\rangle\\
&=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),g(z_{1})\rangle\\
&=\langle\tilde{T},g\rangle,\\
\end{split}

(7.37)

which implies that
i\partial\bar{\partial}\Phi=\tilde{T}
.

Step 3:
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+0}(\inf_{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\Phi(z))=0
and
\inf_{\Omega\backslash U}\Phi>-\infty

Let
W\subset\subset\Omega
be an open set
of
\Omega
which satisfies
\overline{V}\cup\{z_{0}\}\subset W

and
\overline{W}\cap\{-t\leq G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\}=\emptyset
, where
t
is a small enough positive number.
Then for every fixed
z\in\{-t\leq G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\}
,

G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})
is harmonic function on a neighborhood of
\overline{W}
with respect to

z_{1}
.
By the Harnack inequality of harmonic function, there
exists a
M>0
such that

\sup_{z_{1}\in\overline{W}}(-G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1}))\leq M\inf_{z_{1}\in\overline{W}}(-G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})).

As
z\in\{-t\leq G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\}
, we have

Mt>-MG_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq M\inf_{z_{1}\in\overline{W}}(-G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\geq\sup_{z_{1}\in\overline{W}}(-G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\geq 0,

which means that
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+0}(\inf_{{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\times\overline{W}}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1}))=0
.

Note that
0\geq u_{n}(z)=\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle\geq\frac{1}{\pi}\inf_{{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\times\overline{W}}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\|T_{n}\|
holds for any
n
and
z\in\{-t\leq G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\}
, as
\|T_{n}\|=\|\tilde{T}\|<+\infty
and
u_{n}
is decreasingly convergent to
\Phi
, then we have

\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+0}(\inf_{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\Phi(z))\geq\lim_{t\rightarrow 0+0}\frac{1}{\pi}\inf_{{\{G_{\Omega}(z,z_{0})\geq-t\}}\times\overline{W}}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\|\tilde{T}\|=0.

Next, we prove
\inf_{\Omega\backslash U}\Phi>-\infty
.
Note that
p\in V\subset\subset\{G_{\Omega}(z,p)<-t\}\subset\subset U\subset\subset\Omega
, it follows from Lemma
2.13
that there exists a constant
N>0
, such that

G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\geq NG_{\Omega}(z,p)\geq-Nt

(7.38)

holds for any
(z,z_{1})\in(\Omega\backslash U,V)
. As
u_{n}(z)=\langle T_{n}(z_{1}),\frac{1}{\pi}G_{\Omega}(z,z_{1})\rangle
and
suppT_{n}\subset\subset V
for any
n
, then we have
u_{n}(z)\geq-\frac{Nt}{\pi}\|T_{n}\|
hold on
z\in\Omega\backslash U
. Note that
\|T_{n}\|=\|\tilde{T}\|
and
u_{n}
is decreasingly convergent to
\Phi
, then we have
\inf_{\Omega\backslash U}\Phi>-\infty
.

Thus, Lemma
2.14
holds.
